(reduce other rooms)\nC 3.1.1.2 OTHER SIMULTANEOUS CONTRACTS\nDuring the construction period Contracts may be awarded to other Contractors.\nThe Contractor’s attention is drawn to Clause 4.8 of the General Conditions of Contract that\nall reasonable access must be given to other Contractors. The Building Contractor must co-\nordinate his program with the other Contractors to accommodate the overlap of construction\nactivities. No additional payments will be made or the above arrangements.\nC 3.1.2 DESCRIPTION OF THE SITE AND ACCESS\nThe site of the Works is in the residential area of UPINGTON in the Dawid Kruiper Local\nMunicipality area and is accessible by means of tar and gravel roads. Vehicles may only use\nexisting roads and accesses to and on the site. Where no entrance to the construction site\nexists, there may only be moved within the areas as indicated by the Engineer.\nWhere existing roads and accesses are not sufficient for construction purposes, the Contractor\nwill have to construct his own accesses to meet his needs and repair it, to the satisfaction of\nthe Engineer, after completion of the Contract.\nDue to the fact that the works will be executed in residential areas, the Contractor will ensure\nthat his work does not inconvenience the residents. Work will only be executed during normal\nworking hours from Monday to Friday.\nWhere the construction takes place within existing road reserves, the Contractor will at all\ntimes ensure safe passage to vehicles and/or pedestrians.\nWhere only half of the carriageway is accessible to vehicular traffic, sufficient flagmen, signs,\netc. will be provided to safely regulate the traffic. Site traffic management must comply with\nSA Road Traffic Signs Manual, Chapter 13.\nC 3.1.3 NATURE OF SOIL AND UNDERGROUND SOIL CONDITIONS\nThe Geotechnical Report is attached as Annexure C of this tender document.\nThe Contractor is responsible to obtain and supply all material that must comply with the\nminimum requirements for the specific material, as well as the construction and maintenance\nof all access roads to the work on site, to spoil sites and sources of material on site that may\nbe required by the Contractor. No payment will be made for the above and payment will be\ndeemed to be included in the tendered Sum.\nIt is the responsibility of the Tenderers to dig additional trial holes if deemed necessary.\nThe Contractor will be responsible for the obtaining of all materials needed in the construction\nprocess.\nWhere there is not sufficient material available on site, material will have to be obtained from\ncommercial sources.\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 5 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/15/2026 Scope of Works:15/2026\n\nThe information, where applicable, given in this document with regards to the underground\nconditions on site is given in good faith for the convenience of the Tenderer, but must not be\naccepted as representative. The provision of this information does not exempt the Tenderer\nof his primary responsibility of ascertaining the conditions on site for himself.\nC 3.1.4 DRAWINGS\nThe drawings attached in this document are only for tender purposes. Final construction\ndrawings will be issued to the successful contractor at commencement of the project.\nC 3.1.5 CONSTRUCTION PROGRAMME\nC 3.1.5.1 GENERAL\nThe Contractor’s programme to be submitted in terms of Clause 5.6 of the General Conditions\nof Contract shall take full account of all matters as may impact on the sequence of executing\nthe various components of the Works and the requisite rate of progress of the Works, as are\nspecified in or reasonably to be inferred from the Contract.\nC 3.1.5.2 FORMAT\nThe Construction Programme to be submitted by the Contractor in accordance with the\nprovisions of Clause 5.6 of the General Conditions of Contract shall:\na) Be in the form of a bar chart;\nb) Clearly indicate the start and end dates and duration of all construction activities and\nidentify the critical path; and\nc) Take full cognizance of all the Contractor’s risks and obligations in terms of the\nContract.\nd) A resource histogram is required in0line with the submitted program of the works.\nC 3.1.5.3 FAILURE TO MAINTAIN CONSTRUCTION PROGRAMME\nC 3.1.5.3.1 If the Construction Programme has to be revised in terms of Clause 5.6 of the General\nConditions of Contract, because the Contractor is falling behind on his programme, the\nContractor shall submit a revised programme of how he intends to regain lost time to ensure\ncompletion of the Works before the Due Completion Date. Any proposals by the Contractor\nto increase the tempo of work must incorporate positive steps to increase production either by\nthe provision of more labour and plant on the Site, or by using the available labour and plant\nin a more efficient manner.\nC 3.1.5.3.2 Failure on the part of the Contractor to submit or to work according to the programme or\nrevised programmes shall be sufficient reason for the Engineer to take steps to remedy the\nsituation.\nC 3.1.5.3.3 A written request from the Contractor must be obtained for the following inspections: Floor,\nBrickwork, Roof, Practical Completion and Final Completion. Failure to comply with requested\ninspections will result in an R 700-00 (Seven hundred rand) fine and a re-inspection.\nC 3.1.5.4 SPECIFIC PROGRAMME REQUIREMENTS\nThe Contractor’s programme shall also take full account of matters described in the Sub-\nClauses hereunder. No additional payments will be made to the Contractor in respect of any\nadditional costs as it may incur in consequence of arranging or adjusting its programme to\naccommodate the said matters and the Contractor’s various tendered rates and prices shall\nbe deemed to be fully inclusive of such costs.\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 6 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/15/2026 Scope of Works:15/2026\n\nC 3.1.6 SITE ESTABLISHMENT\nC 3.1.6.1 SITE FACILITIES AVAILABLE\nC 3.1.6.1.1 WATER SUPPLY\nThe Contractor shall make his own arrangements with the relevant authorities or any other\norganisation or source for obtaining water for construction and domestic purposes as well as\ntoilet facilities as required by the Health and Safety Regulations. The Contractor shall pay for\nthe water at the rates and tariffs as determined by the source, including the cost of supplying\na temporary standpipe as required. The contractor shall also be responsible for all testing of\nwater by an accredited laboratory, as required.\nC 3.1.6.1.2 ELECTRICITY SUPPLY\nThe Contractor shall make his own arrangements for obtaining power and be responsible for\nall costs involved.\nC 3.1.6.1.3 LOCATION OF CAMP AND DEPOTS\nThe contractor must arrange with the Local Authority for an appropriate site that can be used\nfor the Contractor’s site office and camp.\nC 3.1.6.1.4 HEALTH & SAFETY\nThe contractor must make the necessary arrangements to comply with the Occupational\nHealth and Safety Act. This includes all the registrations required and the appointment of a\nqualified safety officer on site.\nC 3.1.6.1.5 LOCATION OF CAMP AND DEPOTS\nThe Contractor must make his own arrangements for a Camp Site at the location of the\nWorks. The location of the Contractor’s camp, including the material storage areas, will be\nsubject to the Engineer’s approval.\nC 3.1.6.1.6 HOUSING FOR THE CONTRACTOR’S EMPLOYEES\nNo housing is available for the Contractor’s employees, and the Contractor shall make his own\narrangements for housing his employees or transporting them to and from the site.\nThe Contractor is in all respects responsible for the housing and transporting of his employees\nand for the arrangements thereof and no extension of time due to any delays resulting from\nthis, will be granted.\nNo housing on site will be allowed.\nC 3.1.6.2 SITE FACILITIES REQUIRED\nC 3.1.6.2.1 ENGINEERS OFFICE\nA separate office will not be required for the Engineer’s representative.\nThe Contractor will, however, need to furnish an office with a desk, drawing table, a lockable\ncupboard and two chairs for the exclusive use by the Engineer. No telephones need be\nprovided for the Engineer or his staff.\nThe Engineer and his representative shall be allowed free use of all the Contractor’s site\nfacilities.\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 7 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/15/2026 Scope of Works:15/2026\n\nThe Engineer and the Engineer’s representative shall be allowed free use of survey equipment\nand assistants to carry out control work as and when required and the Contractor shall provide\nall pegs, concrete, tools and other necessary items as well as all necessary labour for\nexcavation, bush cleaning, mixing and placing of concrete, as and when required for the\ncontrol of the setting out of the Works.\nC 3.1.6.2.2 SANITATION AND FIRST AID\nThe Contractor shall provide and maintain adequate sanitation and first aid for his work force.\nThese facilities shall comply with the requirements of the Local Authority and must be\naccessible from all points of construction.\nC 3.1.6.2.3 TELEPHONE\nA site telephone will not be required by the engineer but the contractor must be available 24/7\non his cell phone for the duration of the contract. The time related tender rate for the\ncontractor’s telephone shall include for official calls made by/to the Engineer.\nC 3.1.6.2.4 HOUSING FOR THE CONTRACTOR’S EMPLOYEES\nThe contractor shall make his own arrangements with regards to the housing of his employees\nsince no housing is available. The transporting of the contractor’s employees to the site is his\nown responsibility. No extension of time because of mismanagement of afore mentioned will\nbe granted.\nC 3.1.6.2.5 OFFICES FOR THE CONTRACTOR\nThe Contractor must provide for such temporary offices as he may require for his own use.\nNo conference facility is required.\nC 3.1.6.2.6 SITE INSTRUCTION BOOK\nThe Contractor shall provide an A4-size carbon triplicate site instruction book and maintain it\npermanently in the site office. All site instructions to Sub-Contractors will also be recorded in\nthis site instruction book.\nC 3.1.6.2.6 REFUSE\nThe Contractor shall provide suitable refuse containers at his site offices and stores yard. He\nshould ensure that his employees make use of the containers for the disposal of refuse so that\nthe site of the Work as well as the existing production facility will not become polluted. The\nContractor shall dispose of the refuse in the containers at regular intervals in an acceptable\nmanner.\nC 3.1.6.3 FEATURES REQUIRING SPECIAL ATTENTION\nC 3.1.6.3.1 EXISTING SERVICES\nThe Contractor shall take all necessary steps to ascertain the location of existing services\nbefore commencing with any section of the works and shall exercise the greatest care when\nworking near such services.\nThe Contractor shall check the position and level of every existing service before starting any\nconstruction that has to connect with such a service. The Contractor shall give written\nconfirmation of the accuracy or inaccuracy of the positions and levels of existing services.\nThe Contractor shall request the latest available drawings showing the location of services\nalready installed, no more than 3 (three) weeks and not less than 1 (one) week before\ncommencing his operations in any particular area.\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 8 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/15/2026 Scope of Works:15/2026\n\nThe Contractor shall take all the necessary steps to protect any existing services and/or\nstructures whatsoever against damages which may arise as a result of his operations on site.\nThe Contractor shall bear the cost of the repair of damage to any service the possible existence\nof which could reasonably have been ascertained by him in good time and the Contractor shall\nbear the cost of the repair of damage to any structure caused by his operations on site.\nWhere the Contractor is responsible for damage for which repairs have to be carried out by\nthe Employer or an outside authority, the costs of these repairs will be covered by means of\na deduction from the Contractor’s Monthly Payment Certificate.\nIt will be accepted that the Tenderer made provision in his tendered Sum for the\ncost of the above. No additional payment will be made by the Client.\nC 3.1.6.3.2 CONTRACTOR’S CONSTRUCTION PLANT\nIf, during the course of the Contract, the Engineer considers that any item or items of\nconstruction plant are in any way inefficient, unsafe or inadequate to complete the Works\nwithin the Contract period, he shall have the right to call on the Contractor to either:\na) Put the construction plant in order;\nb) Remove such construction plant and replace it with other efficient plant; or\nc) Provide additional similar plant or plant of greater capacity.\nNo additional payment shall be made to the Contractor for expenses incurred in complying\nwith any or all of the above, the cost being deemed to be included in the Contractor’s tendered\nSum.\nC 3.1.6.3.3 KEY PERSONNEL\nThe Contractor shall furnish the Employer and the Engineer with a list of addresses and\ntelephone numbers of key personnel in the Contractor’s organization who may be contacted\nin an emergency both during and outside office working hours in connection with the Works.\nC 3.1.6.3.4 PUBLICATION AND ADVERTISING\nThe Contractor shall not publish, or cause to be published in any papers, articles or information\nrelating to the Works nor permit any advertising mentioning the subject of his Contract and\nhe shall not display or allow his Sub-Contractors to display any advertisement on the site or\nelsewhere in connection with the Works without the prior written permission of the Employer.\nC 3.1.6.3.5 ORDERS AND INDENTS\nOn acceptance of this tender the Contractor is to ascertain if all materials to be supplied by\nhim can be obtained in South Africa and if not, steps will be taken to import the same so that\nthe Works are not delayed. Delay in the Works owing to non-delivery of materials will not be\nconsidered a cause for delay in completing the Works.\nC 3.1.6.3.6 SECURITY AND SAFETY REGULATIONS\nThe Contractor is to familiarize himself and comply with all safety regulations, statutes and\nregulations governing construction activities. The safety of all personnel on site shall be the\nContractor’s sole responsibility.\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 9 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/15/2026 Scope of Works:15/2026\n\nC 3.1.6.3.7 ACCESS TO PROPERTIES\nThe Contractor shall organize the work to cause the least possible inconvenience to the\nmunicipal operating staff. The Contractor will be responsible for the necessary traffic control\nwhere required. Works over private property will be done in close co-operation with the\nspecific Landowners.\nThe Contractor shall ensure that all streets, roads and footpaths adjacent to or crossing the\nsite and which are affected by the works and/or temporary works are kept in a safe condition\nfor pedestrians and vehicular traffic. The Contractor shall organise his work so as to reduce\nthe inconvenience to traffic and the private Landowners to a minimum, and no public road\ntrack or footpath shall be completely closed without prior approval.\nIf so ordered, the Contractor shall provide suitable bridges at street and driveway crossings\nwhere traffic must cross open trenches.\nAll signs shall be in English and Afrikaans.\nC 3.1.6.3.8 SITE MAINTENANCE\nDuring progress of the work and upon completion, the site of the Works shall be kept and left\nin a clean and orderly condition. The Contractor shall store materials and equipment for which\nhe is responsible in an orderly manner and shall keep the site free from debris and obstruction.\nC 3.1.6.3.9 TESTING AND QUALITY CONTROL\nC 3.1.6.3.9.1 CONTRACTOR TO ENGAGE SERVICES OF A SUITABLE LABORATORY\nNotwithstanding the requirements of the Specifications pertaining to testing and quality\ncontrol, the Contractor shall engage the services of a suitable laboratory to undertake all\ntesting of materials, the results of which tests are specified in, or reasonably to be inferred\nfrom the Contract, as to be taken into consideration by the Engineer in deciding on whether\nthe quality of materials utilized and workmanship achieved by the Contractor complies with\nthe requirements of the Specifications. The afore going shall apply irrespective of whether\nthe said testing is indicated in the Specifications as to be carried out by the Engineer or by the\nContractor.\nThe Contractor shall be responsible for arranging with the testing laboratory for the timeous\ncarrying out of all such testing specified in the Contract, at not less than the frequencies and\nin the manner specified. The Contractor shall promptly provide the Engineer with copies of\nthe results of all such testing carried out by the laboratory.\nFor the purposes of this Clause, a “suitable laboratory” shall mean a laboratory which is not\nunder the management or control of the Contractor and in which the Contractor has no\nfinancial interest, nor which has any control of financial interest in the Contractor.\nC 3.1.6.3.9.2 ADDITIONAL TESTING REQUIRED BY THE ENGINEER\nIn addition to the provisions of Sub-Clauses 7.4.2 and 7.4.3 of the General Conditions of\nContract, the Engineer shall be entitled at times during the Contract, to require that the\nContractor arrange with the laboratory to carry out any such tests, additional to those\ndescribed, at such times and at such locations in the Works as the Engineer shall prescribe.\nThe Contractor shall promptly and without delay, arrange with the laboratory for carrying out\nof all such additional testing as required by the Engineer and copies of the test results shall\nbe promptly provided to the Engineer.\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 10 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/15/2026 Scope of Works:15/2026\n\nC 3.1.6.3.9.3 COSTS OF TESTING\n(a) Tests in terms of Sub-Clause 7.4.4.1 of the General Conditions of Contract:\nThe costs of all testing carried out by the laboratory in accordance with the\nrequirements of Sub-Clause 7.4.4.1 of the General Conditions of Contract above shall\nbe borne by the Contractor and shall be deemed to be included in the tendered Sum.\nNo separate payments will be made by the Employer to the Contractor in respect of\nany testing carried out in terms of Sub-Clause 7.4.4.1 of the General Conditions of\nContract.\nWhere, as a result of the consistency of the materials carrying or as a result of failure\nto meet the required Specifications for the work, it becomes necessary to carry out\nadditional tests (e.g. Re-test on rectified work and/or replacement material), the\ncosts of such additional testing shall be for the Contractor’s account.\n(b) Additional tests required by the Engineer\nThe cost of any additional tests required by the Engineer in terms of Sub-Clause\n7.4.4.1 of the General Conditions of Contract shall be reimbursed to the Contractor,\nprovided always that the costs of any such additional tests ordered by the Engineer,\nthe results of which indicate that the quality of the materials utilized and/or the\nstandard of workmanship achieved is not in accordance with the Specifications, shall\nnot be reimbursable to the Contractor.\nNo separate payment will be made for such testing, the cost of which will be deemed\nto be included in the Contractor’s tendered Sum for the items of work that require\ntesting in accordance with the Specifications.\nC 3.1.6.3.10 EXTENSION OF TIME DUE TO ABNORMAL CLIMATIC CONDITIONS\nExtension of time will not be granted for what is considered normal adverse climatic\nconditions. Extension of time will only be grated in case of abnormal rainfall or saturated\nconditions in accordance with the method described as follows:\nThe Contractor must make allowance in his programme for the expected number of working\ndays for which possible delays due to weather conditions may occur as scheduled in the\ntable below.\nExtension of time for each calendar month or part thereof for the full period required for the\ncompletion of the works, plus any approved extensions thereof will be calculated as follows:\nDelays caused by abnormal climatic conditions will only be considered for extension of time\nif, in the opinion of the Engineer, critical path items as indicated on the Contractor’s\nprogramme are influenced negatively. Only delays occurring on working days will be\nconsidered.\nExtension of time will be granted for the number of days on which accepted adverse climatic\nconditions occurred, minus the number of days for the specific month in the schedule\nbelow.\nThe nett extension of time determined for every month, which may be negative, will be\nalgebraically accumulated to determine the total nett number of days of extended time due\nto adverse climatic conditions. A negative balance at the time of completion will not be\ntaken into consideration.\nWhere a portion of a month is involved, a pro rata number of days will be calculated.\nSCHEDULE:\nExpected number of working days which work may be delayed due to adverse weather\nconditions:\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 11 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/15/2026 Scope of Works:15/2026\n\nMONTH DAYS MONTH DAYS\nJanuary 2 July 1\nFebruary 2 August 1\nMarch 2 September 1\nApril 2 October 1\nMay 1 November 2\nJune 1 December 1\nTOTAL: 12 Days\nC 3.1.6.3.9.10.1 The Engineer shall, simultaneously on granting any extension of time in terms of this\nClause, revise the Due Completion Date of the Contract to reflect an extension of time\nhaving been granted in respect of wet climatic conditions, to the extent of the above\ncalculation provided that where such period is negative, the Due Completion Period shall\nnot be revised.\nC 3.1.6.3.10.2 Any extension of time in respect of wet climatic conditions granted in terms of this Clause\nshall not be deemed to take into account delays experienced by the Contractor in repairing\nor reinstating damage to or physical loss of the Works arising from the occurrence of\nabnormal climatic conditions. Extension of time in respect of any such repairs or\nreinstatement of damage shall be the subject of a separate application for extension of time\nin accordance with the provisions of the General Conditions of Contract.\nC 3.1.6.3.11 SUB-CONTRACTORS (NOMINATED OR APPROVED)\nThe Contractor shall be responsible for work carried out by both nominated and approved\nSub-Contractors on his behalf.\nThe Engineer will not liaise directly with such Sub-Contractors. Problems related to\npayments, programming, workmanship, etc. shall be the concern of the Contractor and the\nSub-Contractor, and the Engineer will not become involved.\nC 3.1.6.3.12 STANDING TIME\nStanding time will only be considered when work is suspended by the written order\nof the Engineer. The Contractor shall not be entitled to recover any standing costs unless\nhe provides full details in writing to the Engineer within 48 (forty-eight) hours of the\nEngineers order.\nStanding time will not be considered when work is suspended as a result of inclement\nweather or default on the part of the Contractor.\nC 3.1.6.3.13 LABOUR RETURNS\nThe Contractor shall provide the Engineer with monthly returns showing the number and\ngrade of employers and the number and type of construction plant on site.\nC 3.1.6.3.14 SITE MEETINGS\nThe Engineer shall hold monthly site meetings and keep and circulate minutes. The\nContractor will attend and will ensure that all Sub-Contractors are represented.\nC 3.1.6.3.15 RESTRICTED AREAS\nThe Contractor and his workers shall remain within the demarcated area of the construction\nsite. No persons will in particular be allowed in adjacent areas actively used by the\nLandowners for agricultural operations.\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 12 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/15/2026 Scope of Works:15/2026\n\nC 3.1.6.3.16 INTERFACE WITH OTHER CONTRACTORS (OPERATING / EMERGING\nCONTRACTORS)\nOther Contractors may be operating on the Site in close proximity to the Works during the\nconstruction period. The Contractor is to take cognizance of this and specify what\nrestrictions (if any) are to apply.\nC 3.1.6.3.17 MINIMUM NUISANCES TO PERSONS FROM THE SURROUNDING AREA\nThe Contractor is to ensure that he causes an absolute minimum nuisance to persons from\nthe surrounding area by complying strictly with the following:\na) Work to be executed only between the hours of 07:00 and 18:00.\nb) The works to be continuously and adequately watered as a means of dust suppression.\nC 3.1.6.3.18 BORROW PITS\nNo borrow pits or sand quarries have been specifically allocated for this Contract. The\nContractor is advised to liaise with the Municipality and/or Commercial Quarries on the\navailability of material.\nC 3.1.6.3.19 DEALING WITH WATER\nThe Contractor is responsible for the control of storm water from adjoining areas, the site\nand groundwater. No additional payment will be made and it will be deemed to be included\nin the rates of the relevant items.\nC 3.1.6.3.20 SURVEY BEACONS\nNB: Only if available to the Department of CoGHSTA NC, The Engineer will provide\nbenchmarks with levels and co-ordinates as and when necessary. The Contractor’s\nattention is drawn to Clause 5.1.2 of SABS 1200 A.\nIt is the exclusive responsibility of the Contractor to ensure that land surveyor’s beacons,\nerf pegs and benchmarks are not covered, disturbed or damaged.\nThe Contractor’s attention is drawn to the stipulations of Article 35 and 36 of the Land\nSurvey Act, 1927 (Act 9 of 1927), in which he is held responsible for the safety of all survey\nbeacons and benchmarks and of any plot boundary pegs that are found on the site, as well\nas for the cost involved in the replacement of displaced and/or damaged survey beacons\nand benchmarks or plot boundary pegs by a registered Land Surveyor.\nAfter completion of the contract and before the final take-over certificate is issued, the\nContractor will have to hand in a certificate from a registered land surveyor certifying that\nall survey beacons, benchmarks and erf pegs are in position.\nErf pegs and trigonometrical land surveyor's beacons that are either misplaced or\ndestroyed during the contract must be replaced and installed by a registered land surveyor\nat the Contractor's expense.\nThe Contractor must point out to the Engineer, in writing, any erf pegs that are not in\nposition within 14 (fourteen) days after commencement of the project. Should the\nContractor fail to point out to the Engineer, in writing, any pegs not in position, it will be\naccepted that all pegs are in position.\nIt will be accepted that the Contractor has made provision under Preliminary and General\nto comply with the above. No additional payments will be made to comply with this.\nThe Contractor’s attention is specifically drawn to the stipulation of Sub-Clause 5.1.2 of the\nStandardised Specifications SANS 1200 A.\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 13 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/15/2026 Scope of Works:15/2026\n\nC 3.1.6.3.21 RECORD DRAWINGS\nAs the Woks proceed the Contractor must keep detailed records of all changes to plans.\nThe actual position of all new and existing services must be indicated on the set of drawings\nsupplied free of charge for this purpose.\nThe Certificate of Completion will only be issued once the Engineer has received the record\ndrawings. No separate payment will be made for this and it will be deemed to be included\nin the rates for the relevant items.\nC 3.1.6.3.22 SAFETY\nThe Contractor must take the safety of the residents and their property into account during\nthe planning and execution of the Works. All open trenches, services, material and\nmachines must be protected and clearly marked.\nUnless otherwise permitted by the Engineer in writing, no more trench in any one place\nshall be opened in advance of the pipe laying operation than can be backfilled before the\nend of the normal working day. Open trenches and/or excavations shall be clearly\nbarricaded with rigid orange nylon netting, “Netlon”, or similar approved. Minimum height\n1,0 m (safety netting) as instructed by the Engineer and/or Safety Agent. No trenches\nand/or excavations will be left open outside normal working hours. See Clause 5.8 of the\nGeneral Conditions of Contract. The cost to provide the above will be taken as included\nwith the tendered rates for the excavations. The Employer will make no additional payment\nto comply with the above.\nWhere applicable, no trenches may be left open during the Contractor’s holiday during\nDecember and January. All trenches which have been excavated but which have not been\nfinally backfilled and compacted at the commencement of the said holiday period shall be\ntemporarily fully backfilled and compacted to a standard which will:\na) Prevent damage occurring to the trenches or any other part of the Works;\nb) Prevent damage to or physical loss of the property of any person;\nc) Eliminate the risk of injury to any person during the said period.\nAll costs involved in the temporary backfilling and compaction of such trenches and the\nsubsequent re-opening of the trenches after the holiday period shall be for the Contractor’s\naccount.\nThe Contractor shall comply with all the safety regulations of the Employer, Other\nAuthorities and/or as demanded by the Engineer.\nIt will be accepted that the Contractor has made provision in his tender to comply with\nabovementioned requirements and no additional payment shall be made to comply with\nthese requirements.\nThe Contractor must ensure that works are properly safeguarded with the necessary road\nsigns, chevron safety bands, lights, etc. at all times, especially at night. The Engineer\nreserves the right to instruct the Contractor to supply additional safety measures, without\nadditional payment. Remuneration to comply with these requirements must be included\nin the tendered amounts in Section: Preliminary & General of the Bill of Quantities.\nThe possible cost of shoring of excavation sides must be included in the cost of\nexcavations. The Engineer reserves the right to request shoring or to strengthen any\nshoring without additional remuneration to the Contractor.\nThe “Factories, Machinery and Building Work Act (Act 22 of 1941)” as well as the\n“Machinery and Occupational Safety Act (Act 6 of 1983)” must, where it may appear in the\nSANS 1200 Standardised Specifications, be replaced by the “Occupational Health and\nSafety Act (Act 85 of 1993).\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 14 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/15/2026 Scope of Works:15/2026\n\nC 3.1.6.4 CERTIFICATES OF PAYMENT\nThe statement to be submitted by the Contractor in terms of Clause 6.10 of the General\nConditions of Contract shall be prepared in accordance with the standard payment certificate\nprescribed by the Engineer and shall consist of at least 2 (two) sets of A-4 sized paper copies.\nAll costs resulting from the preparation and submission of the statements shall be borne by\nthe Contractor.\nThe value certified in each payment certificate shall only be based on houses where specific\ninterim payment stage has been reached and a Certificate of Compliance for that part of the\nwork has been issued and signed by the Engineer, considering the information submitted by\nthe Contractor.\nNo interim payments will be made for site establishment, demolishing and discarding\nof materials. First payment will be made only after milestone 1 (Platforms) has been\nreached.\nThe interim payment stages shall be based on the following progress achieved per house:\nINTERIM PROGRESS COMPLETED % CUMULATIVE\nSTAGE PAYMENT %\n1 Completion of platform, completely inspected and\n5 5\napproved by Engineer.\n2 Completion of raft foundation &/or foundation &\nfloor slab construction, completely inspected and 25 30\napproved by Engineer.\n3 Completion of all brickwork up to wall plate height\nincluding all door and window frames built in\n15 45\ncomplete, inspected and approved by the\nEngineer.\n4 Completion of roof trusses and roof sheeting\ncomplete, inspected and approved by the 18 63\nEngineer.\n5 Completion of all plastering, inspected and\n8 71\napproved by the Engineer.\n6 Complete installation of all doors and glazing,\ndisposal, ironmongery and all finishes, inspected 5 76\nand approved by the Engineer.\n7 Complete installation of ceiling with prescribed air\ngap inclusive of above-ceiling insulation, 5 81\ninspected and approved by the Engineer.\n8 Complete plumbing installation, inspected and\n4 85\napproved by the Engineer\n9 Complete electrical installation, inspected and\n5 90\napproved by the Engineer.\n10 Complete casting of aprons as per specifications,\n2 92\ninspected and approved by the Engineer.\n11 Practical Completion – Completion of house in\n8 100\ntotality, inspected and approved by the Engineer\n12 Relocation of existing informal shacks Rate Rate\n13 Removal of existing informal brick structures Rate Rate\n14 Relocation of existing electrical services Rate Rate\nNo re-measuring of quantities will be applicable. See also C 3.1.9.14.\nALL payments are subject to 5% retention. See also C 6.10.3.\nC 3.1.6.5 CONSTRUCTION IN LIMITED AREAS\nAlthough not very often, working space may sometimes be restricted. The construction\nmethod used in these restricted areas largely depends on the Contractor’s plant. However,\nthe Contractor must note that measurement and payment will be according to the specified\ncross-sections and dimensions irrespective of the method used and that the rates and\nprices tendered will be deemed to include full compensation for difficulties encountered\nwhile working in restricted areas. No extra payment or any claim for payment due to these\ndifficulties will be considered. Where, in the Engineer’s opinion, the use of hand excavation\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 15 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/15/2026 Scope of Works:15/2026\n\nhas been deemed necessary, it has been allowed for in a separate item of the Schedule of\nQuantities.\nC 3.1.6.6 SPOIL MATERIAL\nNo indiscriminate spoiling of material will be allowed. All surplus or unsuitable material\nshall be spoiled at a site to be indicated by the Employer. Such site shall meet with the\napproval of the Local Authority within whose area it falls and the spoiling shall comply with\nall the statutory and municipal regulations.\nC 3.1.6.7 SAMPLES\nThe Contractor shall at his own cost, supply all samples that may be required. Material or\nwork not conforming to the approved samples shall be rejected. The Engineer reserves to\nhimself the right to submit samples to any tests to ensure that the material represented by\nthe sample conforms to the requirements of the Specifications.\nC 3.1.6.8 MANUFACTURER’S INSTRUCTIONS\nThe recommendations of the Manufacturers of patented materials must be strictly adhered\nto regarding the use, mixing, application, fastening, etc. thereof except when otherwise\ninstructed by the Engineer in writing.\nC 3.1.6.9 PROPRIETARY MATERIALS\nWhere proprietary materials are specified it is to indicate the quality or type of materials or\narticles required and where the terms “or similar approved” are used in connection with\nproprietary materials or articles, it is to be understood that the approval shall be at the sole\ndiscretion of the Engineer.\nC 3.1.6.10 NOTICES, SIGNS, BARRICADES AND ADVERTISEMENTS\nThe Contractor shall erect the necessary signs, notices and barricades for the duration of\nthe Contract in order to safeguard both the Works and the Public.\nNotices: The Contractor may use signs and barricades as well as advertisements only\nupon approval, and the Contractor shall be responsible for their supply, erection,\nmaintenance and ultimate removal and shall make provision for this in his tendered rates.\nThe Engineer shall have the right to have any sign, notice or advertisement moved to\nanother position or to have it removed from the site of the Works, should it in any way prove\nto be unsatisfactory, inconvenient or dangerous to the general public.\nSuch notices, signs and barricades shall be provided and erected at the Contractor’s own\nexpense.\nC 3.1.6.11 SETTING OUT OF WORK\nBenchmarks and reference line data shall be provided to the Contractor at commencement\nof the Contract. The Contractor shall be responsible for the proper and accurate setting\nout of the Works.\nThe Contractor shall be responsible for the correctness of the position, levels, dimensions\nand alignment of all parts of the Works.\nThe checking of any setting-out of any line or level by the Engineer shall not relieve the\nContractor of his responsibility for the correctness thereof. The Contractor shall be\nresponsible for the provision of all necessary instruments, appliances and labour in\nconnection with his responsibility for setting out of the works.\nIf at any time during the progress of the Works, any error shall appear or arise in the\nposition, level, dimension or alignment on any part of the Works, the Contractor, on being\nrequired to do so by the Engineer, shall rectify such error.\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 16 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/15/2026 Scope of Works:15/2026\n\nC 3.1.6.12 WORKMANSHIP AND QUALITY CONTROL\nThe onus to produce work which conforms in quality and accuracy of detail to the\nrequirements of the Specifications and drawings rest on the Contractor and the Contractor\nshall, at his own expense, institute a quality control system and provide experienced\nEngineers, foremen, surveyors, materials technicians, other technicians and staff, together\nwith the transport, instruments and equipment, to ensure adequate supervision and positive\ncontrol of the Works at all times.\nThe cost of all supervision and process control, including testing thus caries out by the\nContractor shall be deemed to be included in the rates tendered for the related items of\nwork.\nThe Contractor’s attention is drawn to the provisions of the various standardized\nSpecifications regarding the minimum frequency of testing that will be required for process\ncontrol. The Contractor shall, at his own discretion, increase this frequency where\nnecessary to ensure adequate control.\nOn completion of every part of the work and submission thereof to the Engineer for\nexamination, the Contractor shall furnish the Engineer with results of all relevant tests,\nmeasurements and levels to indicate compliance with the Specifications.\nC 3.1.6.13 TRANSPORT OF MATERIAL\nAll costs of transporting material, including overhaul, shall be included in the applicable\ntendered rates. All references in the Specifications to transport, overhaul and haul\ndistances shall be deleted irrespective of whether or not the deletion is included in these\nSpecification Data.\nThe tendered unit prices for the provision of all imported construction materials from feudal\nquarries or commercial sources and for the removal of spoil material and unfit material,\nshall be deemed as including the loading and transportation of material from the source to\nthe final unloading point, as well as the unloading thereof.\nNo additional payments shall be made for any imported construction materials.\nAll haul distances, unless otherwise specified in the Bill of Quantities, shall be deemed as\nfree haul, including the dumping of excess material.\nC 3.1.6.14 EMPLOYMENT OF PREVIOUSLY DISADVANTAGED LOCAL LABOUR\nWhere possible the Contractor must make use of unemployed local labour.\nC 3.1.6.15 LIAISON WITH LOCAL AUTHORITIES\nThe Contractor will have to liaise with the Local Authorities regarding the following matters:\na) Dealing with traffic;\nb) Locating of existing underground services;\nc) Protection of existing services during construction.\nAll relevant authorities will be notified of above operations. It is then the Contractor’s onus\nto immediately contact all these Authorities and to accommodate their involvement in his\nprogramme of work. The Contractor should also warn the Authorities at least 48 (forty-\neight) hours before the actual work commence. Compensation for delays, losses or\naccidents will not be considered should the Contractor at and time have failed to keep the\nLocal Authorities informed. The Engineer and/or Employer must immediately be notified,\nshould the Contractor experience any problem regarding work, which involves a Local\nAuthority.\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 17 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/15/2026 Scope of Works:15/2026\n\nC 3.1.6.16 LOCAL MATERIAL\nWhere possible the Contractor must make use of local suppliers for materials. It shall be\ncompulsory for the contractor to source the required bricks from the local brick suppliers.\nC 3.1.7 WAY LEAVES, PERMISSIONS AND PERMITS\nThe Contractor shall be responsible for obtaining all the necessary way leaves, permissions\nor permits applicable to working near any existing services or other infrastructure on site,\nand shall ensure that any way leaves, permissions or permits obtained by the Employer’s\nAgent prior to the award of the Contract are transferred into the Contractor’s name.\nThe Contractor shall abide by any conditions imposed by such way leaves, permissions or\npermits.\nThe Contractor shall ensure that all way leaves, permissions and permits are kept on site\nand are available for inspection by the relevant service authorities on demand.\nThe Contractor shall also ensure that any way leaves in respect of electricity services are\nrenewed timeously every 3 (three) months.\nC 3.1.8 ALTERNATIVE TENDERS\nIn the case of an Alternative Tender submitted by the Contractor having been accepted by\nthe Employer, the provisions as set out hereunder shall, in addition to the other\nrequirements of the Contract, apply in the Contract.\nC 3.1.8.1 COMPLETION AND SUBMISSION OF FINAL DESIGNS AND DRAWINGS\nC 3.1.8.1.1 The Contractor shall, not later than 1 (one) month prior to the date on which it intends to\ncommence work on the Works or any part thereof which is the subject the Contractor’s\nalternative technical proposals in respect of the design or Specifications of the\nWorks contained in an alternative Tender accepted by the Employer, submit to the\nEngineer for his approval, the complete set of final working drawings, including general\nlayout drawings and bending schedules, final design calculations, Specifications, the\ndesign assumptions and parameters on which the designs are based and all other\ndocumentation and details as may be required by the Engineer for the purposes of\nevaluating and approving the final design, Specifications and drawings.\nC 3.1.8.1.2 The information and details to be submitted by the Contractor shall comply in all respects\nwith the following:\na) Calculations\n(i) Calculations shall include calculations of stresses in the structure as relevant,\nincluding calculations of reinforcing or pre-stressing steel.\n(ii) The calculations shall be set out in a clear and logical manner to facilitate\nchecking.\n(iii) A full description of the design assumptions shall accompany the calculations.\nb) Drawings\n(i) Drawings shall show the whole structure in elevation, sectional elevation and\nin plan to a suitable scale.\n(ii) Sufficient large-scale sections and other details shall be submitted to show\nthe concrete and other dimensions clearly.\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 18 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/15/2026 Scope of Works:15/2026\n\n(iii) Foundation levels and foundation sizes as well as the steel reinforcement at\ncritical sections shall be indicated on the drawings.\n(iv) The standard of detailing and the quality of the prints shall be the same as\nthat of the Contract drawings supplied to the Contractor, or in the absence of\nany such contract drawings having been provided, of the same standard as\nthat which was provided in the Tender Documents.\n(v) The drawings shall be compiled in the official language of the Contract.\nc) Further details\nShould the Engineer conclude that the calculations, drawings, Specifications or any\nother data submitted by the Contractor in accordance with the provisions of this\nClause are insufficient or inadequate for proper evaluation, the Engineer reserves\nthe right to require the Contractor to submit such further calculations, drawings,\nSpecifications and any other such data as the Engineer may require. If such further\ndetails are not submitted within the time required by the Engineer, the Tenderer will\nbe deemed in default of the provisions of this Clause.\nC 3.1.8.1.3 The Contractor shall submit only drawings and other data, which are complete in all\nrespects and in accordance with Sub-Clause C 3.3.5.1.2. If the final calculations, drawings\nand details do not comply with the specified requirements, the alternative designs will be\nrejected unless suitably amended by the Contractor.\nC 3.1.8.1.4 The Contractor will not be entitled to any claim for delays experienced as a result of the\nsubmission of incomplete drawings or other documents and data, which is not strictly in\naccordance with the requirements of this Specification.\nC 3.1.8.1.5 The Contractor shall not commence executing the Works or any portion thereof which is\nthe subject of alternative technical proposals in respect of the design or Specifications of\nthe Works contained in an Alternative Tender accepted by the Employer, until the\nEngineer’s approval of the designs and calculations has been given in writing and the\nDrawings signed by the Employer, or the Engineer on the Employer’s behalf.\nC 3.1.8.2 STATUS OF ACCEPTED DRAWINGS\nC 3.1.8.2.1 The accepted Drawings shall form an integral part of the Contract Documents and drawings\nnot accepted and signed by or on behalf of the Employer will not be permitted for\nconstruction or manufacturing purposes.\nC 3.1.8.2.2 Notwithstanding the approval and/or acceptance and signing of the drawings, the\nContractor shall remain fully responsible for the details, discrepancies, omissions, errors\nand consequences in respect of the said drawings and the approval of a design by the\nEngineer shall not in any way relieve the Contractor of its responsibility to produce a design\nthat complies with all the specified requirements.\nC 3.1.8.3 MEASUREMENT AND PAYMENT\nC 3.1.8.3.1 DESIGN, CONSTRUCTION AND REMEDYING OF DEFECTS\na) Amount\nNo payment will be made to the Contractor in respect of its costs incurred in the\ndesign, preparation and submission of drawings and other documents pertaining to\nan accepted Alternative Tender.\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 19 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/15/2026 Scope of Works:15/2026\n\nb) Re-measurement\nNotwithstanding anything to the contrary as may be contained in the Contract, the\nsaid Works or portions thereof (as applicable) which are the subject of the works\nshall not be subject to re-measurement and the quantities listed by the Contractor in\nthe Schedule of Quantities forming part of its Alternative Tender shall be fixed and\nnot subject to any variation whatever during the Contract.\nc) Interim Payments\nThe amounts which shall become due and payable to the Contractor in the monthly\nPayment Certificates, in respect of the portions of the Works which are the subject\nof the Contractor’s alternative technical proposals, shall be determined on the basis\nof the quantities of work certified as having been completed in the period for which\nthe payment applies as inspected in floor; brick work to roof; roof; Practical\nCompletion and Final Completion; provided always that no payment will be made in\nrespect of quantities exceeding those listed by the Contractor in the said Schedule.\nWhen payments on the abovementioned stages have been made per house the next\npayment must be Practical Completion or payment might be stopped until such time\nthat said houses are practical complete.\nC 3.1.8.3.2 ENGINEER’S COSTS IN REVIEWING THE CONTRACTOR’S DESIGN\nThe Engineer’s costs incurred in reviewing, checking and approving the designs, drawings,\ncalculations and other documents pertaining to the Contractor’s accepted Alternative\nTender (and which designs, drawings, calculations and other documents were submitted\nby the Contractor in accordance with the provisions of both the Tender Documents and the\nContract) shall, on presentation of an account to the Contractor and certified in writing by\nthe Employer, be paid by the Contractor to the Engineer.\nThe Contractor shall be reimbursed in the actual amounts of all such payments made in\nthe next subsequent Payment Certificate, in substitution of the Provisional Sum provided\nby the Contractor in the Schedule of Quantities forming part of its Alternative Tender in\naccordance with the requirements of the Tender Documents.\nC 3.1.8.4 VARIATIONS TO THE ACCEPTED ALTERNATIVE PROPOSALS\nC 3.1.8.4.1 VARIATIONS BY THE ENGINEER\na) When the Engineer requires design modifications for reasons other than:\n(i) The Contractor’s failure to comply with the design requirements; or\n(ii) Errors in the Contractor’s designs (e.g. foundation conditions that differ materially\nfrom those indicated by the test holes); the Contractor shall make such\nmodifications.\nb) When such design modifications result in a variation on the quantities of work to be\nexecuted, such variations will be valued by the Engineer in accordance with the rates\nand prices in the Schedule of Quantities and the tendered Lump Sum for the alternative\nwill be adjusted up or down, depending on whether the modifications entail an increase\nor a decrease in the quantity of work.\nC 3.1.8.4.2 VARIATIONS BY THE CONTRACTOR\nThe Contractor shall not, subsequent to the approval of its alternative designs,\nspecifications and drawings, amend without the prior written permission of the\nEngineer.\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 20 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/15/2026 Scope of Works:15/2026\n\nC 3.1.8.5 DEFAULT OF THE CONTRACTOR\nC 3.1.8.5.1 Should it become apparent at any time during construction or during the Defects Liability\nPeriod that the Contractor’s alternative design and/or Specifications do not comply with the\nspecified requirements, the Contractor shall be liable for all consequential damage and\nshall, at its own expense, do all the work required to ensure that the structure complies with\nthe design requirements and the Contractor shall not be entitled to any additional payment.\nC 3.1.8.5.2 When circumstances that are within the control of the Contractor arise after the acceptance\nof the Alternative Tender and when these circumstances, in the opinion of the Engineer,\nrender construction of the alternative unacceptable, the Contractor shall construct the\nWorks strictly in accordance with the original designs specified in the Tender Documents.\nIn such circumstances, the Contractor shall not be entitled to any additional payment.\nC 3.1.9 GENERAL\nC 3.1.9.1 CONNECTION TO EXISTING WORKS\nWhere construction has to be connected to existing works, the Contractor must negotiate\na suitable time for the connecting with the Employer. It may be necessary to connect after\nhours and/or over weekends. Tendered prices for connection must provide for the above\nand/or control of all flows and no additional payments will be made by the Employer to\ncomply with this.\nC 3.1.9.2 CLEANING OF PIPES AND STRUCTURES\nThe Contractor shall ensure that pipes and structures shall be clear of all planks, stones,\nconcrete, etc. which may be found in them, before commissioning. Any damage caused to\nequipment, structures, etc. as a result of the presence of above objects, shall be repaired\nat the Contractor’s cost.\nC 3.1.9.3 BLASTING\nWhere the Contractor is going to make use of blasting in excavations, the Contractor must\nnotify all residents in the vicinity of the works in writing at least 14 (fourteen) days before\ncommencement of the works of the proposed blasting.\nThe Contractor must also inspect all buildings in the vicinity at least 14 (fourteen) days\nbefore commencement of the blasting and any existing damage to buildings must be noted.\nOwners must sign the noted inspection within 7 (seven) days. After completion of all\nblasting in the vicinity, the buildings must once again be inspected, and damage noted.\nThe Owners must again sign the noted inspection.\nThe Employer shall not be held accountable for any damages caused due to the use of\nexplosives.\nC 3.1.9.4 BEDDING AND BACKFILL\nPlacing and compacting of bedding and filling around pipes, as well as the main backfill\nover pipes, shall be done strictly according to the requirements of SANS 1200, the Project\nSpecifications, Details on Drawings and the Specifications of the pipe manufacturers. No\ndeviation of the above will be allowed. Tenderers shall make adequate provision in their\ntendered unit prices to comply with the above in full. The Employer shall make no\nadditional payments to comply with the above.\nBackfill density shall be done strictly in accordance with SANS 1200 DB, Sub-Clauses\n5.7.1 and 5.7.2. The Contractor shall execute control tests as and when so requested by\nthe Engineer, to verify densities, as prescribed in SANS 1200. Cost of the test will be\ndeemed as included in bedding and backfill tendered rates.\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 21 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/15/2026 Scope of Works:15/2026\n\nC 3.1.9.5 CONTRADICTIONS AND OBSCURITIES IN THE TENDER DOCUMENT\nShould there be any contradictions, obscurities or doubt in the text of the Tender Document\nor drawings, or if any obvious errors or illegible figures are found, the Contractor must,\nbefore submitting the tender, get a written, signed declaration of the correct meaning of\nsuch descriptions, figures, Clauses, etc. from the Engineer.\nThe Contractor shall not be permitted to submit any claims against the Employer and/or\nEngineer after closing of tender due to the abovementioned reasons.\nThe Contractor must examine the Tender Documents to ensure that it contains all the\napplicable pages and that a complete set of drawings has been issued. The Contractor\nmust notify the Engineer accordingly should there be any pages and/or drawings missing.\nThe Engineer shall then immediately supply a complete set of Tender Documents and/or\ndrawings in exchange for the incomplete set of Tender Documents and/or drawings.\nThe Contractor will be forced to, at own cost, repair all work caused by the incorrect\ninterpretation of the drawings and /or Specifications and as a result not complying with the\nrequirements of this contract document and/or drawings.\nC 3.1.9.6 CONTRACTOR TO BE COMPENSATED\n(CLAUSE 4.5.4 OF THE GENERAL CONDITIONS OF CONTRACT)\nThe Employer shall NOT refund to the contractor any such sums.\nC 3.1.9.7 VESTING OF MATERIAL\nAll material on site is to remain the contractor’s responsibility throughout the duration of the\ncontract. The contractor is to ensure that all material had to be stored in a safe and secure\nlocation until completion of the works at hand.\nC 3.1.9.8 SPECIFICATIONS\nWhere referred to SANS Specifications or any other specification, it shall mean the latest\npublished edition of abovementioned specification.\nC 3.1.9.9 COSTS INCURRED WITH THE PREPARATION AND SUBMISSION OF TENDERS\nThe Employer shall not be held responsible for any expenses or losses incurred by the\nTenderer with regards to the preparation and/or submission of tender documents.\nC 3.1.9.10 STATUTORY LAWS AND REGULATIONS\nThe Contractor must supply all notices and pay all fees, as required by an Act of the\nParliament, Ordinance or any Regulations or By-Laws or any Local or other Statutory\nPower with regards to the executing of the Contract Works or any other Temporary Works\nand according to the Employer and all Public Authorities and Companies whose property\nor rights may at any time be influenced by the Contract Works or any temporary Works.\nThe Contractor shall under all circumstances comply with the regulations of any Act of\nParliament, Ordinance or any Regulations or By-Laws or any Local or other Statutory\nPower that may be applicable to the Contract Works or any Temporary Works and\naccording to rules and regulations of the Employer and all Public Authorities and\nCompanies, as already mentioned, and shall indemnify the Employer against all fines and\nresponsibilities of any kind for breach of contract of such an Act, Ordinance, Regulation or\nBy-Laws.\nThe Employer shall compensate the Contractor, or reimburse the Contractor for all amounts\npayable, as specified by the Engineer, that is payable and already paid for by the Contractor\non such fees, and also all taxes paid by the Contractor for the Site or any part thereof or\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 22 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/15/2026 Scope of Works:15/2026\n\nanything constructed or erected on site, or any part thereof, or any temporary structure,\nplaced elsewhere, but exclusively for the use of the Contract Works, with the understanding\nthat the Employer, and not the Contractor, shall be responsible for the obtaining any\nplanning permissions that may be required for the Contract Works.\nC 3.1.9.11 LAWS THAT SHALL APPLY\nThe contract shall at all times be executed in accordance with the Laws of the Republic of\nSouth Africa, and any discrepancies that may occur between the Employer and the\nContractor as far as the contract is concerned, shall be settled in the Republic of South\nAfrica, unless the Law on the contract applicable on this Clause is from another country,\nthe Employer is entitled to adopt the Law from another country in the case of any\ndisagreement and/or the case may be settled in such a country.\nAll Laws and/or Regulations referred to in the Tender Document will be, where applicable,\n“as amended”.\nC 3.1.9.12 LABOUR\nC 3.1.9.12.1 CONTRACTS WITH LABOURERS\nThe contractor will enter into employment contracts with all labourers to be employed during\nthe construction period of this project.\nC 3.1.9.12.2 WAGE RATES\nWage rates for labourers will be paid in accordance with the latest Government Gazette for\nthe Northern Cape Province. The current rate is R29.37/hour for a normal 9 hours working\nday.\nC 3.1.9.12.3 COMMUNITY LIAISON OFFICER (CLO)\nA community liaison officer (CLO) will be appointed on a full-time basis for the duration of the\nproject in order to facilitate, inter alia, the smooth proceeding of the employment of local labour.\nThe CLO will be the link between contractor and labourers. The CLO will be identified by the\nLocal Municipality but will be paid out of the project. The wages for the CLO shall be in line\nwith the Local Municipality CLO labour wage rate/month for an average 9 hour working day.\nC 3.1.9.12.4 LABOUR INTENSIVE CONSTRUCTION ACTIVITIES\nThe following activities where applicable shall be carried out using Labour Intensive\nConstruction (LIC) methods:\n1. Preparation of bedding and blanket\n2. Laying of all pipes with a diameter of less than 355mm\n3. Installation of all fittings and accessories (valves etc.) to pipes\n4. Mixing and placing of concrete for small concrete works (i.e. thrust blocks)\n5. Building of manholes and benching\n6. Finishing and cleaning of site\n7. Other activities that by their nature are usually done by labour intensive construction\nmethods.\nThe Contractor is encouraged to add activities to the above list but he shall ensure that the\nspecified standards of construction will be achieved.\nAlthough it is the intention that the above activities be carried out by labour intensive\nconstruction methods the Contractor may propose to the Engineer alternative ways in which\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 23 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/15/2026 Scope of Works:15/2026\n\nthe work is to be executed. The Engineer’s approval of these alternative methods will not be\nunreasonably withheld from the Contractor.\nC 3.1.9.12.5 EMPLOYMENT OF LOCAL LABOUR\nC 3.1.9.12.5.1 QUANTUM OF KEY PERSONNEL\nThe Contractor must submit the description and numbers of his key-personnel that he will bring\nonto Site in a Key Personnel Schedule. Key personnel will include Foremen, Artisans, Clerks,\nskilled Supervisors and Operators.\nC 3.1.9.12.5.2 QUANTUM OF LABOUR EMPLOYED\nThe Contractor shall submit detailed daily labour records, weekly, to the Engineer indicating\nrespectively the numbers of permanent and temporary local employees employed on the\nWorks, and the activities on which they were engaged.\nThe number of labourers stated by the Contractor in the Key Personnel Schedule shall be\nused by the PLO (if appointed), Engineer and Employer, in collaboration with the Contractor\nin the planning and programming of the Contractor’s local labour requirements.\nC 3.1.9.12.5.3 PAYMENT AND PRODUCTIVITY\nPayment to the local labour force shall be made on a forth nightly basis in respect of Tasks\ncompleted during that period. Formal up to date records must be kept of all payments made\nto subcontractors and labourers.\nIn order that the project is economically viable and the employment of labour is not merely a\n“hand-out” to the local community, is important that payment of the labour force is linked to\nproductivity. Increased productivity can be achieved by utilising the “Task Work” Principal (see\nClause PS 9.7), in terms of which the Contractor will be required to reward the labour force on\nthe basis of Tasks completed.\nC 3.1.9.12.6 CONDITIONS OF TEMPORARY EMPLOYMENT\nIt is envisaged that there may not be sufficient experienced local subcontractors available to\nwarrant tenders or quotations on the base of competitive labour rates. Equally it may prove\nconfusing to the local labourers and therefore counterproductive for possible tenderers to\nbargain for lower labour rates. A rate agreed upon at tender stage, may no longer be accepted\nas valid by the time the Tender is awarded. For the purpose of this tender therefore, tenders\nare to price labour at the approved local minimum daily rate as prescribed by the Department\nof Labour.\nThe rate of payment to local labour will be based on the accepted contractual productivity\nlevels. The Engineers Representative will monitor productivity to ensure that this Principal is\ncarried out. For labour intensive construction (LIC) activities where no production rate is\napplicable, the minimum rate of payment per working day specified above shall apply.\nThe following conditions of work shall complement the conditions of employment:\n(1) The Contractor shall give to a temporary employee, at the earliest possible\nopportunity, notice of the termination of the project and/or the requirements of that\nemployee’s participation in the project; provided that such notice.\n(2) The temporary employee shall, upon termination of his services, be entitled to a\ncertificate of service showing the full names of the employer and the employee, the\ndate of commencement, a record of training received and the date of termination of\nthe contract.\n(3) Skilled labour e.g. Brick layers and carpenters will be paid normal hourly rates as\ncommonly used in the industry.\n(4) The Contractor must provide unemployment insurance for the local labourers.\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 24 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/15/2026 Scope of Works:15/2026\n\nC 3.1.9.12.7 TASK WORK RELATED ACTIVITIES\nA task shall be determined on the basis of what an average person from the local Community\ncould complete in a day. A Task shall be defined on the said basis with regard to the prevailing\nphysical conditions and other regulatory conditions as specified in Clause PS 9.6.\nA task is a quantified activity or operation to be performed by a person/labourer in one ordinary\nworking day. The quantification of tasks shall be based on individual employees or a group of\nemployees. The supply and control of hand tools and other equipment necessary to do the\nwork, will be the responsibility of the Contractor.\nDESCRIPTION UNIT QUANTITY\n1. Excavation in:\n- Soft (sandy) material\n0 to 1,0 m deep m³ 3,0\n0 to 1,5 m deep m³ 2,2\n1. Backfilling: 0 to 1,5 m deep m³ 6\n2. Brickwork to Manholes and similar (220 mm thick) m² 5\n3. Pipe laying, including bedding and blanket\n4.1 Sewer Pipes m 48m/team of 8\n4.2 Water Pipes m 60m/team of 8\nThe activity and production rates ranges given in the schedule above, must be used only as a\nguideline.\nC 3.1.9.12.8 TRAINING OF LOCAL LABOUR\nC 3.1.9.12.8.1 IN SERVICE TRAINING OF LOCAL LABOUR\nThrough the core of artisans, skilled and semi-skilled personnel are required to construct,\nsupervise and adequately control the Contract; the Contractor shall provide the necessary in-\nservice (on-the-job) training in basic construction skills.\nC 3.1.9.13 INTERCHANGEABLE TERMS\nThroughout this document the terms “Bid/Bidder” and “Tender/Tenderer” has the same\nmeaning.\n“Bill of Quantities” and “Schedule of Quantities” will also have the same meaning.\nC 3.1.9.14 BILL OF QUANTITIES: LUMP SUM CONTRACT\nThe Tender is a Lump Sum per Bill of Quantities Sections and no re-measuring of\nquantities will be applicable.\nThe Bill of Quantities is included in the document to assist Tenderers to price the Works.\nQuantities are provided in good faith, but Tenderers must ensure that their tendered prices\nfor the works are all inclusive for the works as indicated on the drawings and as per the\nSpecifications.\nThe Client will not pay any additional amounts not included in the Tendered amounts.\nDue to the fact that the Bill of Quantities is not re-measurable, monthly payments will be\ncalculated in accordance to C 3.1.6.4.\nThe Bill of Quantities were compiled as per “Model Preambles for Trades: 2008”. A copy\ncan be viewed at the Offices of the Consulting Engineer.\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 25 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/15/2026 Scope of Works:15/2026\n\nC 3.1.9.15 VALUE ADDED TAX\nThe Contractor must be registered as a vendor in terms of the Law on Value Added Tax of\n1. NOTE THAT COGHSTA DOES NOT PAY VALUE ADDED TAX.\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 26 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/15/2026 Scope of Works:15/2026\n\nC 3.1.10 MINIMUM SPECIFICATIONS FOR THE HOUSES\nC 3.1.10.1 INTRODUCTION\nTenderers must determine their independent tendered Fixed Rate per house, but\nthe pricing structure of the houses must be in line with the National Human\nSettlement Guidelines.\nHouse type and /or sizes are as listed below per area:\nStandard 40m² House = 12 units\n45m² Disabled House = 0 units\nC 3.1.10.3 MINIMUM SPECIFICATIONS\nC 3.1.10.3.1 Listed below are some abstracts from the SANS 10400 and also the specifications from\nNational Housing Code, the specifications and code will be judged as the minimum\nrequirements for these houses:\nC 3.1.10.3.1.1 The minimum standard is a 40m² house, consisting of a lounge/kitchen, 2 bedrooms and a\nbathroom;\nC 3.1.10.3.1.2 The installation of a ceiling with the prescribed air gap for the entire dwelling;\nC 3.1.10.3.1.3 The installation of above-ceiling insulation comprising a 135 mm mineral fibreglass blanket for\nthe entire house;\nC 3.1.10.3.1.4 Internal walls to be plastered and painted (1 coat universal pva primer and 2 coats matt durable\npaint.)\nC 3.1.10.3.1.5 A standard basic electric installation comprising a pre-paid meter-ready board with a recessed\ndistribution board with lid and lights and double plugs to all living areas of the house, water-\ntight outside lights above the front and back doors, and stove point in the kitchen area.\nInclusive of Earthing and earthing peg.\nThe Electrical Installation must be done by a person registered as an Electrical Contractor in\nterms of the Occupational Health and Safety Act of 1993 - “Electrical Installation Regulations”,\nas well as be registered with the Local Council / Authorities;\nC 3.1.10.3.1.6 Must comply with the latest NHBRC specifications; SANS 10400 and SANS 1200.\nC 3.1.10.3.1.7 Special low E clear and opaque safety glass of all windows.\nC 3.1.10.3.2 Construction Specifications:\nC 3.1.10.3.2.1 Walls:\n(i) “Brickforce” of internal walls to fully overlap that of the external walls. Masonry walls to\nhave 2.8mm dia suitable “Brickforce” every third course and in every course above window\nheight and in foundation walls.\n(ii) Internal walls: plastered 12mm, 1:6 cement plaster with 1 x plaster primer and 2 x pure\nacrylic emulsion paint (Colour – Natural Wicker) .\n(iii) External walls: plastered 12mm, 1:6 cement plaster with 1 x plaster primer and 2 x Exterior\npure acrylic emulsion paint (Colours – Chalk Stone, Beige Sand and Cape Fog).\n(iv) Jointing to be done on the external doors and internal doors as per Engineers drawings.\n(v) All bricks of the external walls to be 140x90x290mm cement maxi brick with a minimum\ncompression strength of 14MPa.\n(vi) All bricks of the internal walls to be 140x90x290mm cement maxi brick with a minimum\ncompression strength of 14MPa.\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 27 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/15/2026 Scope of Works:15/2026\n\nC 3.1.10.3.2.2 Doors:\n(i) 2.0 mm Steel cottage section windows and 1.2 mm thick steel doorframes with fixed lugs\nneatly built into brickwork. Windows to have standard fittings.\n(ii) Install precast lintels over all window- and door openings.\n(iii) Exterior 2032 x 813 x 44 mm FL&B (Meranti or Saligna) door complete with 70 mm\nweather board and 3-lever lock set.\n(iv) Internal 2032 x 813 x 44 mm hollow core doors (commercial veneer) to be fitted with 2-\nlever lock set. Doors to have standard fittings.\n(v) Meranti or similar approved weather strip on external doors\nC 3.1.10.3.2.3 Windows:\n(i) Kitchen Windows: C7 with 4 mm glass (No. = 1)\n(ii) Bedroom Windows: D7 with 4 mm glass (No. = 2)\n(iii) Lounge Windows: D57 with 4 mm glass (No. = 1)\n(iv) Bathroom Windows: ES1 with 4 mm obscure glass (No. 1)\n(v) All glazing to comply with SABS 0137\nC 3.1.10.3.2.4 Plumbing:\n(i) All plumbing to be carried out by a Plumber registered with the Local Council/Authorities.\n(ii) Build-in 1700 mm bath side wall and sand bed complete with 15 mm chromium plated hot\nand cold taps, 40 mm bath outlet and removable fibre cement inspection panel. Neatly\nsilicone along wall surfaces;\n(iii) Fit one basin complete with 2 x pillar taps, plug with chain chromed outlet and PVC trap.\nNeatly silicone along wall surfaces.\n(iv) Fit one WC complete with 6 ℓ cistern, fitments, toilet seat and flush pipe.\n(v) Provide and fit a 1200 x 535 mm single bowl sink unit with one chromium plated bib tap,\n38 mm chromed waste outlet and 40 mm PVC trap, on one pair of brackets. Neatly\nsilicone along wall surfaces.\n(vi) Hot and cold water polycop lines to be neatly chased into walls to all sanitary fittings. Hot\nwater to be blanked off in roof for future geyser.\n(vii) Install angle valves and a stop cocks where applicable.\n(viii) A 16 mm HDPE water connection to the Municipal network, inclusive of a stopcock.\nC 3.1.10.3.2.5 Drainage:\n(i) Provide 1 x 15 mm bibtap over a gully at the kitchen.\n(ii) Provide a 110 mm vent valve at the head of the 110 mm soil line.\n(iii) Waste water pipe system to have 50 mm vent valve at highest point.\n(iv) Provide a marked rodding eye at the head of the soil drain and at all change of direction.\nAll pipe connections on soil drain to have inspection eyes.\nC 3.1.10.3.2.6 Finishes:\n(i) Floors to be power-floated to a smooth and level finish and kept damp for a period, as per\nEngineer’s requirements, before any brickwork commences.\n(ii) Steel window- and door frames to have a 1 x coat red oxide factory coated primer, 1 x\nuniversal undercoat and 2 x finishing coats.\n(iii) External timber doors: well sanded and cleaned, apply 1 coat wood stain and 2 coats\nexternal polyurethane varnish.\n(iv) Internal doors: well sanded and cleaned, apply 1 x coat timber primer and 1 x universal\nundercoat and 2 coats Enamel paint.\n(v) Window panes to be fitted with correctly prepared putty and only painted when surface is\nfirm and dry - not to be left unpainted for too long, paint as per window frames.\n(vi) House numbers on Perspex plaque 150mm High Black on Silver\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 28 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/15/2026 Scope of Works:15/2026\n\nC 3.1.10.3.2.7 Roof:\n(i) Certified pre-fabricated nail plate timber roof trusses;\n(ii) Minimum roof slope of 17.5 degrees.\n(iii) Roof covering to be 0.5 mm corrugated cranked chromadek roof cladding, color sandstone\nbeige, installed according to manufacturer's specification.\n(iv) 30x1.2mm Galvanised hoop iron roof anchors at 1050mm centres on the eaves and on\nthe centre wall including on each rafter to the gable walls.\n(v) 220x12mm fibre cement fascias and barge boards to truss ends and eaves with 50x5mm\ncounter sunk steel wood screw heads and apply paint as for exterior walls.\n(vi) All sprockets to be treated with carbolineum or similar approved product.\nC 3.1.10.3.2.8 Ceiling:\n(i) 6.4mm Gypsum Ceiling with R value of 3.38m² K/W on 38x38mm timber branderings\n@450mm centres one direction.\n(ii) 16mm Aluminium Joiner H-strips.\n(iii) Standard 70mm coved gypsum cornice fixed with “Crestestone” OSA paste. Apply acrylic\nfiller where needed and 2 x white commercial PVA paint.\n(iv) 135mm Mineral fibreglass blanket insulation with R Value of 0.062m² K/W above ceiling.\n(v) All ceiling to be installed in accordance to SANS 10400.\nC 3.1.10.3.2.9 Aprons:\n(i) Concrete strength of 25MPa\n(ii) Maximum slump of 75mm\n(iii) 85mm thick, 1000mm wide\n(iv) Maximum length of panels: 2500mm with 10mm expansion joints\nC 3.1.10.4 COMPLETION QUALITY PACK:\nThe contractor will be required to submit a Completion Quality Pack for each stand number in\nthe scope of works as part of completion of the project.\nEach pack will consist of the following documentation:\n(i) Signed Happy Letter\n(ii) SANS 10400 – A: Form 4 (Structure) – Signed by the Implementing Agent\n(iii) Test Cube results\n(iv) Test Results and certification where applicable\n(v) Soil Poisoning Certificate\n(vi) Roof Erection Certificate\n(vii) COC – Electrical Installation\n(viii) COC – Plumbing Installation\n(ix) COC – Glazing Installation\n(x) Timber Truss Manufacturer Guarantee\n(xi) Roof Sheeting Manufacturer Guarantee\nAll testing to be done by a SANAS accredited laboratory\nRetention payments will be retained should the above documents not be submitted per stand.\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - The Engineer may in his discretion require any\nmaterial not bearing such mark to be tested in accordance with the relevant Specifications;\nshould he do so, the Contractor shall arrange for such tests to be carried out, to the\nContractor’s cost, by the South African Bureau of Standards or other approved body.\nWhether or not the material bears the mark or is tested, any material found not to be in\naccordance with the Specifications shall be rejected and replaced by the Contractor at his own\ncost.\nThe Contractor may be required, at his own expense, to submit samples of the material offered\nto the Engineer for his approval and the material supplied under his contract shall be of the\nstandard equal to that of the samples so submitted and approved. It shall be kept in full working order and repair at all times.”\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 36 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/15/2026 Scope of Works:15/2026\n\nA 5 CONSTRUCTION\nA 5.1 SURVEY\nPS A 5.1.1 SETTING OUT OF THE WORKS\nADD THE FOLLOWING TO SUB-CLAUSE 5.1.1:\n“Setting out of works shall not be measured and paid directly and any costs involved herein\nshall be deemed as included in the tendered Sum of work that is included in the Contract.\nFurther to the above, the Contractor shall timeously, before commencement of construction,\ncheck all levels in a specific area to provide for both the accurate measurement of quantities\nfor payment purposes and should any discrepancies occur, to give the Engineer ample time\nto check levels and to make adjustments, if necessary.\nShould the Contractor neglect to do so, the levels as shown on the Engineers drawings will be\nused for measurement and payment purposes.\nSetting out details of all the works are defined by offsets from pegs and benchmarks\nestablished by the Engineer. The Contractor shall be responsible for the setting out of the\nworks with reference to these pegs and benchmarks. The Contractor will survey the area after\ncompletion of the works and supply the data to the Engineer.”\nPS A 5.1.2 PRESERVATION AND REPLACEMENT OF SURVEY BEACONS AND PEGS SUBJECT TO\nTHE LAND SURVEY ACT\nDELETE THE WORDS “in the vicinity of boundaries” IN THE SECOND SENTENCE OF SUB-\nCLAUSE 5.1.2 AND REPLACE THE WORDS: “under the direction of” IN THE SAME\nSENTENCE WITH “in consultation and liaison with.”\nADD THE FOLLOWING AFTER THE SECOND SENTENCE OF SUB-CLAUSE 5.1.2:\n“The Contractor and the Engineer shall record on the said list, their concurrence or\ndisagreement, as the case may be, regarding the completeness and accuracy of the details\nrecorded therein.”\nREPLACE THE THIRD SENTENCE OF SUB-CLAUSE 5.1.2 WITH THE FOLLOWING:\n“At the completion of the Contract, the Contractor shall expose all pegs that were listed at the\ncommencement of the construction as being in order and the Contractor shall arrange with a\nregistered Land Surveyor for the checking of the positions of all such pegs and the\nreplacement of any thereof which the Land Surveyor’s check reveals have become disturbed\nor damaged.
